<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Media">Media</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea-Bissau&amp;action=edit&amp;section=45" title="Edit section: Media">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Media_of_Guinea-Bissau" class="mw-redirect" title="Media of Guinea-Bissau">Media of Guinea-Bissau</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Music">Music</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea-Bissau&amp;action=edit&amp;section=46" title="Edit section: Music">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Music_of_Guinea-Bissau" title="Music of Guinea-Bissau">Music of Guinea-Bissau</a></div>
<p>The music of Guinea-Bissau is usually associated with the <a href="/wiki/Polyrhythm" title="Polyrhythm">polyrhythmic</a> <a href="/wiki/Gumbe" title="Gumbe">gumbe</a> <a href="/wiki/Musical_genre" class="mw-redirect" title="Musical genre">genre</a>, the country's primary musical export. However, civil unrest and other factors have combined over the years to keep gumbe, and other genres, out of mainstream audiences, even in generally syncretist African countries.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Cabasa" title="Cabasa">cabasa</a> is the primary <a href="/wiki/Musical_instrument" title="Musical instrument">musical instrument</a> of Guinea-Bissau,<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> and is used in extremely swift and rhythmically complex <a href="/wiki/Dance_music" title="Dance music">dance music</a>. Lyrics are almost always in <a href="/wiki/Guinea-Bissau_Creole" title="Guinea-Bissau Creole">Guinea-Bissau Creole</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_language" title="Portuguese language">Portuguese</a>-based <a href="/wiki/Creole_language" title="Creole language">creole language</a>, and are often humorous and topical, revolving around current events and controversies.<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The word <i>gumbe</i> is sometimes used generically, to refer to any music of the country, although it most specifically refers to a unique style that fuses about ten of the country's <a href="/wiki/Folk_music" title="Folk music">folk music</a> traditions.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Music_of_Guinea-Bissau" title="Music of Guinea-Bissau">Tina and tinga</a> are other popular genres, while extent folk traditions include ceremonial music used in funerals, <a href="/wiki/Initiation" title="Initiation">initiations</a>, and other rituals, as well as <a href="/wiki/Balanta_people" title="Balanta people">Balanta</a> brosca and kussundé, <a href="/wiki/Mandinka_people" title="Mandinka people">Mandinga</a> djambadon, and the kundere sound of the <a href="/wiki/Bissagos_Islands" title="Bissagos Islands">Bissagos Islands</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cuisine">Cuisine</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea-Bissau&amp;action=edit&amp;section=47" title="Edit section: Cuisine">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Cuisine_of_Guinea-Bissau" class="mw-redirect" title="Cuisine of Guinea-Bissau">Cuisine of Guinea-Bissau</a></div>
<p>Common dishes include <a href="/wiki/Soup" title="Soup">soups</a> and <a href="/wiki/Stew" title="Stew">stews</a>. Common ingredients include <a href="/wiki/Yam_(vegetable)" title="Yam (vegetable)">yams</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sweet_potato" title="Sweet potato">sweet potato</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cassava" title="Cassava">cassava</a>, onion, tomato, and <a href="/wiki/Cooking_banana" title="Cooking banana">plantain</a>. Spices, peppers, and chilis are used in cooking, including <i><a href="/wiki/Aframomum_melegueta" title="Aframomum melegueta">Aframomum melegueta</a></i> seeds (Guinea pepper).<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Film">Film</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea-Bissau&amp;action=edit&amp;section=48" title="Edit section: Film">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p><a href="/wiki/Flora_Gomes" title="Flora Gomes">Flora Gomes</a> is an internationally renowned film director; his most famous film is <i><a href="/wiki/Nha_Fala" class="mw-redirect" title="Nha Fala">Nha Fala</a></i> (English: <span lang="en">My Voice</span>).<sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup> Gomes's <i><a href="/wiki/Mortu_Nega" title="Mortu Nega">Mortu Nega</a></i> (<i>Death Denied</i>) (1988)<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> was the first fiction film and the second feature film ever made in Guinea-Bissau. (The first feature film was <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=N%E2%80%99tturudu&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="N’tturudu (page does not exist)">N’tturudu</a></i>, by director <a href="/w/index.php?title=Umban_u%E2%80%99Kest&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Umban u’Kest (page does not exist)">Umban u’Kest</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umba%C3%B1_U_Kset" class="extiw" title="fr:Umbañ U Kset">fr</a>&#93;</span> in 1987.) At <a href="/wiki/FESPACO" class="mw-redirect" title="FESPACO">FESPACO</a> 1989, <i>Mortu Nega</i> won the prestigious Oumarou Ganda Prize. In 1992, Gomes directed <i><a href="/wiki/Udju_Azul_di_Yonta" class="mw-redirect" title="Udju Azul di Yonta">Udju Azul di Yonta</a></i>,<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> which was screened in the <a href="/wiki/Un_Certain_Regard" title="Un Certain Regard">Un Certain Regard</a> section at the <a href="/wiki/1992_Cannes_Film_Festival" title="1992 Cannes Film Festival">1992 Cannes Film Festival</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-festival-cannes.com_135-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-festival-cannes.com-135">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> Gomes has also served on the boards of many Africa-centric film festivals.<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> The actress <a href="/wiki/Babetida_Sadjo" title="Babetida Sadjo">Babetida Sadjo</a> was born in <a href="/wiki/Bafat%C3%A1" title="Bafatá">Bafatá</a>, Guinea-Bissau.<sup id="cite_ref-rtbf_137-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rtbf-137">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sports">Sports</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea-Bissau&amp;action=edit&amp;section=49" title="Edit section: Sports">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Football is the most popular sport in Guinea-Bissau. The <a href="/wiki/Guinea-Bissau_national_football_team" title="Guinea-Bissau national football team">Guinea-Bissau national football team</a> is controlled by the <a href="/wiki/Football_Federation_of_Guinea-Bissau" title="Football Federation of Guinea-Bissau">Federação de Futebol da Guiné-Bissau</a>. They are a member of the <a href="/wiki/Confederation_of_African_Football" title="Confederation of African Football">Confederation of African Football</a> (CAF) and FIFA.
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Quinara_FC" title="Quinara FC">Quinara FC</a></div>
